```text
Goal: Create an ultra-detailed photorealistic isometric miniature scale-model diorama poster of the world from 
Attack on Titan
, presented like a collectible museum map on a clean off-white studio background.
Canvas: Vertical 4:5 poster composition. The upper third is typography, the lower two-thirds is a floating square-cut terrain diorama with rugged rocky sides and a soft realistic shadow beneath.
Top typography: Centered at the top, use tall condensed bold distressed sans-serif lettering. Small spaced text above reads "THE WORLD OF". Main title reads "
ATTACK ON TITAN
" in very large dark charcoal letters. Small spaced text below reads "
WIT STUDIO • 2013
".
Diorama layout: Show the walled civilization as a high-angle isometric model with three enormous concentric circular stone walls and fortified gates. The center contains a dense circular royal city with radial streets and a white central monument. Surrounding rings contain miniature villages, farms, roads, rivers, forests, watchtowers, and tiny human figures. Beyond the walls at the back are snow-capped mountains, wilderness, scattered ruins, and open terrain. The outer edge of the model is a thick cliff-like cutaway rock base with grass on top.
Labeled signs: Include exactly 14 small dark rectangular map-label plaques with white uppercase text, placed around the diorama: 1) "BEYOND THE WALLS" near the mountains, 2) "ZIGANSHINA DISTRICT" on the upper-left wall area, 3) "KROLVA DISTRICT" on the upper-right wall area, 4) "SHIGANSHINA DISTRICT" on the far right, 5) "LAKE NALLE" by a blue lake on the left, 6) "YARCKEL DISTRICT" on the left outer area, 7) "CHLORBA DISTRICT" on the lower-left area, 8) "HUMANITY'S STRONGEST" beside a small military crest on the lower-left, 9) "TROST DISTRICT" on the right outer area, 10) "TRAINING GROUNDS" on the lower-right, 11) "ROYAL CAPITAL MITRAS" at the central city, 12) "INNER WALL WALL SINA" on the innermost wall, 13) "MIDDLE WALL WALL ROSE" on the middle wall, 14) "OUTER WALL WALL MARIA" on the front outer wall.

Add a few tiny Titan-like humanoid silhouettes outside and near the walls for scale, but keep the scene map-like rather than action-focused.
Visual style: Hyper-realistic miniature model photography mixed with anime-world cartography, crisp isometric perspective, warm daylight, realistic shadows, high texture detail, tiny hand-painted model figures, natural greens and browns, pale stone walls, snowy gray mountains, cinematic depth of field but all important labels legible.
Constraints: Keep the image as a single poster, no border, no watermark, no extra logos. Preserve the exact 14 map labels listed above and the three wall labels. Use an off-white background and a centered floating diorama with clean studio lighting.
```

## 例 721：Healing Woodcut Print Poster

**来源：** [YouMind](https://youmind.com/prompts/healing-woodcut-print-poster-32442)

![case721.jpg](images/case721.jpg)

```text
Please create an independent high-end design poster for each photo I upload, with no multi-photo stitching; each photo should be output separately. The overall composition uses a 3:4 vertical layout, with the upper and lower areas strictly at a 1:1 height ratio, each occupying 50% of the frame. The upper part retains the original photo, maintaining the subject's identity, structure, posture, spatial relationships, authentic materials, natural light and shadow, and original color atmosphere, with only slight high-end photographic color grading to give it the texture of an independent magazine, art publication, or exhibition photography. To adapt to the frame, the sky, ground, or environmental background can be naturally extended, but the subject must not be stretched, distorted, mirrored, or altered. The lower part extracts the most recognizable subject, outline, posture, and narrative relationship from the photo, reconstructing it as a limited-color woodcut print mark suspended on paper. Do not directly copy the entire photo or simply apply a filter; instead, actively prune complex backgrounds and trivial details, keeping only the outlines, proportions, directions, light and dark zones, key structures, and core spatial relationships that determine recognizability. Re-summarize the subject using the language of woodcut, linocut, and hand-stamped printing to make it instantly identifiable while possessing a rustic, clumsy, and strongly handcrafted artistic temperament. The graphic brushstrokes should carry the characteristic clumsiness and handmade traces of woodcut prints: lines need not be smooth or precise, allowing for slight hesitations like knife carvings, uneven thickness, fuzzy edges, stiff transitions, local fractures, gaps, misalignments, and irregular negative space; the edges of color blocks can show dry brushing, missing prints, ink shortages, and coarse-grained textures. The overall style is not a refined vector illustration but feels as if it were carved out stroke by stroke with a hand chisel and ink, carrying a simple, direct, slightly naive yet very durable printmaking quality. The composition centers on a single visual anchor point, large areas of negative space, and incomplete edges. Position and scale are determined by the subject's own visual center of gravity, action direction, and structural flow—it can be centered, offset, slightly cropped, or extended in a certain direction. Ample warm paper negative space is preserved around the subject, with edges gradually disappearing through ink gaps, fractured textures, paper transparency, and natural wear, avoiding a complete rectangular border so the image feels like a memory gently pressed onto the paper. The color scheme extracts the most recognizable and vibrant colors from the photo above but transforms them into a 'little fresh,' soft, healing, and bright limited-color logic. Use clean and light comprehensive color systems such as cream white, ivory white, pale pink, misty green, light blue, pale yellow, light orange, mint green, soft coral, and light ochre, maintaining low to medium saturation, high brightness, and soft contrast. Retain the most moving color memory points of the original image but avoid dark, dull, dirty, heavy, and overly retro color schemes. All colors should appear as if matte solid ink were printed layer by layer, soft and clear, yet still retaining the grain and warmth of a print. Text is also integrated as part of the print composition rather than fixed descriptive text. Extract a main title of 1-3 words from the subject's identity, action, emotion, state, location, or metaphor in the photo, paired with a set of very short keywords or a miniature description. The main title can be arranged along the subject's outline, negative space boundaries, visual axes, or print edges, forming a supporting, interleaving, misaligned, or echoing relationship with the graphic; auxiliary small text remains tiny and restrained, using typewriter fonts, monospaced serif fonts, or old-archive-style layouts, like notes in a travel diary or a private print collection. Do not use fixed years; let the text grow naturally based on the photo itself. The overall emphasis is on the rustic handmade feel of woodcut prints, fresh and soft limited colors, paper negative space, incomplete edges, and restrained layout, presenting a warm, quiet, healing, light, nostalgic but not dark high-end print temperament. Whether the subject is a person, animal, plant, architecture, object, vehicle, or natural landscape, a clear and clever visual echo should be maintained between the real photography above and the print reconstruction below, avoiding ordinary filters, complete background replicas, smooth vector outlines, cartoon illustrations, travel template vibes, and cheap retro feelings.
```

## 例 722：3D Papercraft Miniature Poster Design

**来源：** [YouMind](https://youmind.com/prompts/papercraft-miniature-diorama-poster-32429)

![case722.jpg](images/case722.jpg)

```text
Please transform each photo I upload into a separate, standalone premium-designed poster. Do not create multi-image collages or combine multiple photos into one composition. Each photo must be output individually. Use an overall 3:4 vertical format, divided into two sections with a strict 1:1 height ratio, with the upper and lower sections each occupying exactly 50% of the canvas. The upper half should retain the original photograph. Preserve the subject’s structure, realistic texture, natural lighting and shadows, and the original color atmosphere. Apply only subtle, sophisticated photographic color grading to give the image the refined visual quality of an art magazine, independent publication, or exhibition photography. To adapt the photograph to the required aspect ratio, you may naturally extend the sky, ground, or surrounding environment, but do not stretch, distort, reshape, or alter the main subject. In the lower half, extract the most recognizable subject, silhouette, pose, and narrative relationship from the photograph, and reinterpret them as a refined miniature 3D paper-craft diorama. Do not mechanically reproduce the photograph. Instead, transform the main subject and a limited number of elements that best reinforce its identity and environmental character into miniature handcrafted models made from materials such as paper, cardstock, soft clay, thin wood pieces, or similar tactile craft materials. Arrange them naturally on a narrow, lightweight, floating landscape base. The main subject must remain instantly recognizable at a glance. All other elements should function only as narrative support. Depending on the original photograph, you may incorporate plants, roads, water, lamps, vehicles, people, birds, clouds, or other representative details of everyday life, but every added element must be genuinely relevant to the original theme. Avoid decorative clutter or meaningless accumulation of objects. Base the composition on one core subject + one horizontal miniature landscape strip + generous negative space. Adjust vertical levels, foreground/background relationships, visual density, and left-right balance according to the subject’s scale, direction, and spatial relationships, so that the scene feels like a carefully curated miniature art installation. Create depth through variations in scale, layered overlaps, and small foreground and background elements. The primary subject should carry the greatest visual weight, while supporting models remain smaller and visually quieter. Keep the overall composition centered without making it excessively symmetrical, maintaining a light, refined, calm, and elegant visual rhythm. Continue using a soft, airy, slightly desaturated premium color palette. Establish the main atmosphere with pale powder blue, mist blue, sky blue, and cool airy blues. Balance these with ivory white, creamy white, light beige, soft gray-green, sage green, and neutral architectural tones. Use only small accents of dusty rose or muted blush for subtle detail. The colors should feel soft yet clean. Avoid muddy gray tones, excessive yellow casts, or candy-like colors. Allow different paper layers to create natural spatial separation through subtle variations in tone and color. Emphasize the authenticity of handcrafted materials. Make paper fibers, folded edges, cut edges, layered thickness, slight fraying, and tiny handmade imperfections visible. Use soft natural diffused lighting and delicate shadows to reveal the real volume, depth, and layering of the miniature models, with the intimate tactile quality of macro photography. Keep the background soft white or an extremely pale paper tone, with extensive clean negative space, so that the entire lower half feels like a meticulously photographed premium handcrafted travel postcard. Typography should also be integrated into the visual language of the miniature paper-craft world. Derive a short English title from the location, identity of the subject, theme, or emotional atmosphere of the photograph. The title does not have to be a city name. Use a small, refined typeface with a subtle handwritten character. Preferably place the title within the negative space below the miniature landscape strip. Depending on the relationship with the subject, the text may also follow a slight curve, align gently along the base, or interact spatially with the miniature scene. Keep the typography minimal, elegant, and restrained. Do not add long descriptive copy. The text should feel more like an artist’s signature than a commercial headline. The overall visual direction should combine the premium qualities of miniature diorama, handcrafted paper art, whimsical 3D illustration, and a premium travel postcard. The result should feel light, gentle, refined, tactile, and full of lived-in character, while maintaining strong design discipline and abundant breathing room. Whether the original photograph features a person, animal, plant, architecture, object, vehicle, or natural landscape, maintain a clear yet clever visual correspondence between the real photographic world in the upper half and the miniature handcrafted world in the lower half. Avoid plastic-looking 3D rendering, toy-like aesthetics, childish craft styles, overly complicated compositions, excessive cuteness, visual clutter, or an e-commerce product-display appearance.
```

## 例 723：Regional Cultural Atlas Poster

**来源：** [YouMind](https://youmind.com/prompts/regional-cultural-atlas-poster-32847)

![case723.jpg](images/case723.jpg)

```text
2x2 grid, 16:9, do this for 4 under rated breakfasts of Southern hemisphere: INPUT = [
TOPIC_CATEGORY
 / 
REGION_SET
 / 
SELECTION_CRITERIA
 / 
MOOD
]
MODE = no_hardcoding / inference_only
Run REGIONAL_CULTURAL_ATLAS_EXTRACTION:
CATEGORY_DNA := infer what type of thing is being compared, how it is used, why it matters, and what makes it visually recognizable
REGION_DNA[each] := infer local environment, visual culture, palette, typography attitude, textures, symbols, landscapes, tools, rituals, and social context
ITEM_SELECTION[each] := infer one overlooked or representative item that fits TOPIC_CATEGORY + REGION + SELECTION_CRITERIA
HERO_VISUAL[each] := infer the best visual treatment:
plated food, product close-up, ritual scene, tool layout, garment portrait, object still life, landscape-backed artifact, map-backed diagram, or symbolic vignette
COPY_SYSTEM[each] := generate:
- large item title
- short emotional tagline
- origin/location label
- 2–4 tiny descriptors
- stamp or badge phrase
- small contextual note
GRAPHIC_SYSTEM := infer torn paper, printed grid, muted color blocks, topographic lines, stamps, borders, halftone, atlas marks, and editorial spacing
Render:
A premium regional comparison poster where each panel feels locally specific but belongs to one unified cultural theme.
Forbidden:
hard-coded Southern Hemisphere, fixed breakfasts, fixed foods, fixed countries, fixed beige/red/green palette, fake local facts, random travel-poster clichés.
```

## 例 727：稚拙民艺感左右分屏叙事海报设计

**来源：** [@xiaoxiaodong01](https://x.com/xiaoxiaodong01/status/2093627864973295787) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/naive-folk-art-side-by-side-narrative-poster)

![case727.jpg](images/case727.jpg)

```text
Please turn each photo I upload into an individual high-end design poster, no multi-photo collages, output each photo separately. Use a 7:6 aspect ratio with a side-by-side layout (not top-bottom), where the two areas are strictly 1:1 in width, each occupying 50% of the frame.

One half preserves the original photo, maintaining the subject's identity, structure, posture, authentic texture, natural light and shadow, and the original color atmosphere, with only slight high-end color grading to give it the texture of an art magazine, independent publication, or exhibition image. To fit the frame, the environment background can be naturally extended, but the subject must not be stretched, distorted, or altered.

The other half extracts the most recognizable subject, silhouette, posture, and narrative relationship from the photo, reconstructing it into a Naïve folk-inspired narrative illustration / Naïve folk-style textured flat narrative illustration. Do not fully trace the photo; instead, actively subtract details, retelling the original scene with silhouettes, primitive forms, a few color blocks, and iconographic elements, keeping only the visual clues that best maintain the subject's identity and story relationship, making the original object instantly recognizable while having a sense of clumsiness, humor, and life.

The subject's modeling centers on simplified silhouettes and large primitive forms. People, animals, buildings, plants, objects, or vehicles can have their proportions moderately compressed, structures summarized, and features exaggerated, without pursuing strict perspective and anatomy. The space uses flattened perspective, establishing narrative through height, overlapping, size, and color block relationships; auxiliary objects are distributed rhythmically like visual symbols, and complex backgrounds are actively deleted, letting a core action, relationship, or scene carry the main story.

The image uses grainy textures like crayons, oil pastels, thick pencils, and dry materials. Black or dark areas should not be filled uniformly but should retain traces of wiping, friction, layering, breakage, and the paper showing through; silhouettes have slight manual errors and a sense of clumsiness, and edges do not need to be absolutely neat. The overall look remains flat, without using realistic light and shadow, metallic materials, complex volumes, or smooth digital vectors.

The color scheme is reorganized by selecting the most vivid, memorable colors that best represent the subject's spirit from the photo, forming a concise and bright limited color palette. Overall, increase the cleanliness and vitality of colors, using warm white paper and dark silhouettes as a stable base, then naturally extracting a few spot-color accents like blue, red, yellow, green, pink, or orange from the original image for clothing, plants, food, architectural parts, or narrative focuses. Colors are distinct but not over-saturated, forming rhythm through large-area light-dark contrast and small-area pop colors, avoiding gray, muddy, Morandi-style, fluorescent colors, and cheap candy-like effects.

Composition emphasizes narrative center, visual weight, positive/negative shapes, size rhythm, and white space relationships. The subject does not need to be centered; it can be offset, partially cropped, or form a loose triangular relationship with auxiliary elements; leave enough blank space so that the rough silhouettes and small colored elements have room to breathe. From a distance, the structure is clear; up close, manual grains and slightly naive stylistic fun are visible, rather than filling all elements evenly.

A small amount of text is involved, without presetting fixed titles, locations, numbers, or languages, freely extracting a small amount of meaningful text based on the photo's subject, action, life state, emotion, or cultural context. Use lightweight, simple folk-art editorial fonts with slight manual printing deviations and irregular character spacing, which can be paired with a small amount of handwriting, making the text naturally exist like signs, menus, notes, or publication annotations in the scene. Text can be arranged staggered along the subject, desktop, wall, white space, or scene structure, participating in the narrative together with graphics, but do not form a regular commercial title bar.

The overall presentation is a visual temperament composed of naïve silhouettes, flat space, crayon grains, limited colors, folk art symbols, and editorial narrative: intimate, humorous, simple, smart, with a sense of life, while maintaining mature composition and design awareness. Avoid children's cartoons, cute stickers, realistic illustrations, smooth vectors, complex perspective, fine facial features, excessive details, 3D rendering, e-commerce illustration style, and templated effects.
——————
We changed it to a side-by-side layout, not top-bottom. Ratio 7:6.
```

## 例 728：高级食材观察旅行盘编辑海报

**来源：** [@Hamburgerai](https://x.com/Hamburgerai/status/2093564437110354121) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/culinary-observation-travel-plate-poster)

![case728.jpg](images/case728.jpg)

```text
Please turn each photo of local cuisine I upload into an independent, high-end "Culinary Observation Travel Plate" editorial poster. Output each photo separately; it is forbidden to stitch multiple photos, different plates, or different dishes into the same work.

The input photo should have a complete plate or bowl as the clear subject, containing at least three identifiable components, such as staple foods, meat, seafood, eggs, vegetables, herbs, nuts, pickles, sauces, or decorative garnishes.

The overall composition is a 3:4 vertical layout, forming a clear contrast between the original food photography and the culinary observation transcription.

The upper part occupies about 45%—50% of the frame, faithfully preserving the original photo. Accurately maintain the outline of the plate or bowl, the number of ingredients, placement direction, area relationships, real texture, natural light and shadow, observation angle, and original colors. Only perform slight color grading suitable for independent food magazines and travel publications, and add a very thin film grain.

Do not re-plate, replace ingredients, move food, change portions, add decorations, or redraw real photography as illustrations in the photo area. When adapting to the frame, environment cropping can be used with restraint, but the main body of the complete plate must remain clearly identifiable.

Use a straight, clear, and restrained horizontal boundary between the upper and lower areas; a very narrow warm-toned paper gap can be kept. No gradients, feathering, dissolving, or letting ingredients cross the boundary.

The lower part occupies about 50%—55% of the frame, using warm off-white or ivory natural drawing paper, showing paper fibers, grain, slight stains, and irregular surfaces.

The lower part should not redraw the entire dish from above in the same composition; instead, decompose the same dish into a sparse culinary observation record on paper:

1. Select 3—6 truly visible and clearly identifiable components from the original photo;
2. In the center or lower part of the bottom section, draw a highly generalized but still instantly recognizable small image of the complete plate or bowl;
3. The small complete plate usually only occupies 28%—40% of the width of the lower part;
4. Extract the selected ingredients from the plate respectively, representing them as independent gouache samples, cross-sections, leaves, grain clusters, sauce color blocks, or structural sketches;
5. Use different scales and directions for different ingredients, forming a sparse, not completely symmetrical observational relationship around the small plate;
6. Use thin and slightly hesitant black ink lines, arrows, and numbers to map the ingredient samples back to their approximate positions in the plate;
7. Keep at least 35%—50% continuous off-white paper whitespace around, do not let arrows, text, and ingredients fill the entire lower area.

The lower part uses the material language of hand-painted culinary field notes:

- matte opaque gouache
- restrained black ink contours
- dry brush gaps
- uneven pigment coverage
- visible paper grain
- imperfect hand-painted edges
- subtle pencil construction marks
- handmade culinary field notebook

Forms should be highly generalized, yet still retain the most recognizable colors, outlines, cross-sections, and grain structures of the ingredients. Do not realistically copy, and do not draw as cartoon food icons.

Colors must be extracted from the original photo, compressing complex colors into 4—6 low to medium saturation ingredient-true colors. The background remains off-white, warm gray, or a very light photo-related color. Allow one warm color like sauce, egg yolk, chili, or fruit to be the visual focus, but avoid neon colors and high-saturation commercial menu color palettes.

Text must be determined word-for-word in the prompt based on photo facts, defaulting to include:

- A cuisine title of 1—3 English words;
- A location and observation number field, such as "THAILAND / PLATE STUDY 01";
- A taste or ingredient observation sentence of 5—12 English words;
- 3—6 short numbered labels, such as "01 RICE", "02 EGG", "03 HERBS".

If specific ingredients cannot be reliably identified, do not invent names; you can use: "RICE", "NOODLES", "GREENS", "SAUCE", "GRILLED ITEM", "PICKLED ITEM".

Text uses narrow handwritten recipe fonts, old recipe lead types, or gentle humanist sans-serif fonts, colored in warm black, deep taupe, or dark colors from the photo. Allow slight ink breakage, paper tooth, and manual errors.

The main title must be clearly readable at chat preview size; numbers and ingredient labels can be smaller but must not shrink into decorative thin lines. Text should be concentrated in a continuous piece of whitespace, like an observer's notes written on paper, rather than smooth digital layout overlaid later.

The overall feel should be like a traveler re-deconstructing, observing, and recording a whole plate of food after tasting a local dish: restrained, warm, clear, with a handmade feel, and also carrying a bit of taste memory and travel archive temperament.

Mandatory avoidance: Photos without a complete single-plate subject, multi-dish tables, multi-plate collages, restaurants or chefs as protagonists, fictional ingredients, adding ingredients not in the photo, proportional redrawing of the entire plate, realistic menu illustrations, dense nutritional infographics, cartoon food, smooth vector icons, plastic 3D food, full-bleed backgrounds, excessive text, prices, brands, logos, author names, signatures, dates, QR codes, URLs, social media interfaces, gibberish, fake Chinese, and watermarks.
```

## 例 730：牛皮纸负形地标漏印对比海报

**来源：** [@Hamburgerai](https://x.com/Hamburgerai/status/2092632741657677999) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/kraft-negative-stencil-landmark-poster)

![case730.jpg](images/case730.jpg)

```text
Please turn every travel photo I upload into an individual high-end contrast poster. Do not use multi-image splicing; output each photo separately.

Style Name: KRAFT NEGATIVE-STENCIL LANDMARK | Kraft Paper Negative-Stencil Landmark Printing

## Applicable Objects

Suitable for travel landmarks with clear individual silhouettes, such as:

Lighthouses, towers, churches, temples, gate towers, statues, monuments, windmills, solitary boats, pavilions, bridge towers, or architecture with distinct outlines.

The subject should be clearly separated from the sky, water, square, or relatively simple environment.

Do not process all photos into fixed icons like lighthouses, churches, or the same type of travel icon. Do not add birds, boats, trees, people, railings, or decorative landscapes that do not exist in the original photo.

## Overall Layout

Adopt a 3:4 vertical top-bottom contrast composition:

- The upper part (approximately 45%—52%) preserves the high-fidelity original photo
- The lower part (approximately 48%—55%) undergoes a kraft paper negative-stencil conversion
- Use a straight, clear, and restrained horizontal boundary between the two areas
- No feathering, no mutual penetration, and no thick division lines
- Do not generate picture frames, walls, handheld displays, or Mockups

The finished product must retain a substantial and clearly identifiable original photo contrast area. Do not shrink the original image into a small corner icon, nor make the entire poster a stencil print.

## Upper Original Photo

Faithfully preserve the original photo's:

- Landmark identity
- Number of subjects
- Subject orientation
- Proportions of architecture or objects
- Top, base, and key outlines
- Positions of doors, windows, and major structures
- Observation angle
- Number and position of people
- Relationships between sky, ground, water, and space
- Real materials, light and shadow, and original color atmosphere

Apply only slight color grading suitable for high-end travel magazines, architectural publications, or photography exhibitions, and add a very thin film grain.

Do not illustrate the original photo; do not move the subject, reshape the building, replace the sky, add or remove people, stretch, mirror, or duplicate the landmark.

## Lower Kraft Paper

The lower part is a continuous blank field of coarse fiber paper.

Choose a dark color for the paper base based on the temperament of the original photo:

- Natural kraft brown
- Dark taupe
- Cold brown
- Deep indigo
- Dark green
- A single dark color extracted from the shadows of the original photo

Express real natural paper fibers, tiny scraps of paper, slight creases, local color variations, and a matte rough surface.

The paper can look slightly old, but it should not be heavily charred, tattered, covered in stains, or made into a cheap retro background.

## Negative Stencil Mechanism

Remake the subject in the photo as a handmade negative-stencil, coarse-mesh silk screen, or old template print.

First lay down a complete dark coarse paper base, then use only one light-colored opaque ink to "leak" the subject out as if from the paper surface.

Light-colored inks can use:

- Warm ivory
- Off-white
- Light sand
- An extremely light corresponding color extracted from the photo's subject

The entire conversion is strictly limited to two colors or nearly two colors:

Paper base color + light stencil ink.

Do not add a third accent color, and do not use multicolor overprinting, gradients, translucent colors, realistic shadows, or glowing effects.

## Semantic Extraction

Do not copy the full scene of the photo.

Keep only:

- Complete outer silhouette of the subject
- Subject orientation
- Shape of the top and base
- 2—6 internal holes that determine identity
- Connection of the subject with the ground, shoreline, rocks, or water
- Very few subsidiary structures that actually exist in the original photo and help with identification

Remove:

- Complex window panes
- Brick joints and wall textures
- Decorative carvings
- Dense vegetation
- Cloud details
- Reflections
- Realistic light and shadow
- Repetitive figures
- Irrelevant environment
- Photographic noise

The subject must be recognizable at a glance, but internal information should be compressed to a minimum.

Recognition comes primarily from a single continuous block of light-colored negative space, rather than thin lines, cross-hatching, or photo light and dark.

## Composition Method

Adopt a full-frame impactful secondary composition.

The subject occupies the lower area:

- Height approximately 55%—85%
- Width approximately 28%—68%

The subject can be centered or slightly offset according to the center of gravity of the original photo. Allow the base or partial outlines to slightly bleed out of the frame.

Do not perform a two-color redraw at the same scale and complete framing as the photo above. The lower part should significantly enlarge the subject, remove the background, and change the information density.

Keep a continuous, quiet blank field of coarse paper around the subject.

The environment can retain at most one minimalist, intermittent negative space of ground, shoreline, rock mass, or water surface. Do not create a complete landscape background.

## Stencil Texture

The light-colored subject's outer outline should be solid and clear, while carrying slight paper fiber burrs formed by real template printing.

Inside the ink, the following are allowed:

- Uneven coverage
- Random ink missing
- A small number of pinholes
- Local scraping
- Friction wear
- Kraft paper fibers showing through the bottom
- Slight ink leakage or breaks at the template edges

These marks must appear naturally, randomly, and locally.

Do not use uniform grain filters, regular noise, regular grids, or photo threshold effects.

The upper part of the subject, the top outline, and structures determining identity should be relatively complete.

The closer to the bottom of the subject, the more obviously the ink can break, fall off, and gradually be swallowed by paper fibers, creating a feeling that memory is fading.

The bottom fading must be formed by ink loss, wear, and the paper base showing through.

Do not use:

- Smoke feathering
- Soft focus fading
- Regular halftone gradients
- Pixel dissipation
- Rectangular mosaics
- Digital airbrush effects

The print as a whole remains matte, flat, and almost without thickness.

Do not make it into a paper carving, relief, impasto, resin, or 3D model.

## Text

No text is generated by default to let the negative subject maintain maximum graphic impact.

If text is absolutely necessary, only allow:

- One reliable, extremely short location word
- One simple number

The text must use the same light-colored ink and be placed in the edge blank space as a small-area ink-missing stencil print.

Prohibit long paragraphs, author names, signatures, accounts, brands, logos, URLs, QR codes, watermarks, fake Chinese, or gibberish.

## Overall Mood

The overall feel should be like an old landmark stencil print hand-brushed on coarse kraft paper:

Restrained, coarse, quiet, isolated, flat, modern, while having the imperfections left by real paper and manual plate making.

It is not an ordinary silhouette, nor a photo with a two-color filter applied.

The most important visual mechanisms are:

Large blocks of light-colored negative space
+
Minimal internal structural holes
+
Random ink missing showing the base
+
Bottom gradually breaking and fading

## Mandatory Avoidance

Avoid:

ordinary silhouette, vector icon, travel logo, photo threshold filter, automatic tracing, monochrome photo filter, detailed etching, copperplate hatching, dense woodcut lines, halftone gradient, regular noise texture, pixel dissolve, airbrush fade, transparent watercolor, paper relief, paper sculpture, impasto, realistic shadows, plastic 3D, multicolor overprint, complete background coverage, same-crop same-scale redraw, invented landmark, added birds, added trees, added boats, excessive typography, author name, logo, watermark, QR code, gibberish text.

Both the clear original photo and the kraft paper negative-stencil conversion must appear simultaneously; otherwise, it is not considered complete.
```

## 例 733：大胆社论海报超大字体动态交互

**来源：** [@MrLarus](https://x.com/MrLarus/status/2092034848718274659) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/bold-editorial-campaign-poster-dynamic-typography)

![case733.jpg](images/case733.jpg)

```text
Create a bold editorial campaign poster where oversized typography reacts to a physical force created by the subject or product.

Brand: [BRAND NAME]
Product / Campaign: [PRODUCT OR CAMPAIGN]
Main Word: [SHORT WORD]
Subject: [PERSON / PRODUCT / BOTH]
Action: [STEP / JUMP / PULL / SKATE / ETC.]
Force: [PRESS / PUSH / WRAP / SLIDE / OTHER]
Force Direction: [DOWN / UP / ROTATE / DIAGONAL / OTHER]
Main Color: [COLOR]
Accent Color: [COLOR]
Aspect Ratio: [9:16]

Use realistic commercial photography, oversized typography, high-contrast colors, and contemporary editorial design.

Make the main word extremely large, filling roughly 50–75% of the frame. The typography should visibly respond to the chosen force instead of remaining static.

For example:

 PRESS: compress, bend, or shift the letters at the point of impact.
 PUSH: stretch the typography along an upward or forward direction.
 WRAP: let large letterforms pass behind and in front of the subject to create an enclosing motion.
 SLIDE: offset and shift the letters along one strong directional axis.

The deformation must follow the subject’s actual movement and force direction. Keep the typography graphic and readable — avoid turning it into soft 3D, liquid, or cartoon lettering.

Create clear foreground–subject–background interaction so the person or product feels physically connected to the type.

Use one dominant high-impact color with black, white, and a contrasting accent color. Add only a few meaningful campaign details such as the brand name, product name, slogan, specifications, labels, or small editorial text.

Keep people and products realistic, with believable proportions, materials, lighting, shadows, and dynamic movement.

The final image should feel like a finished campaign key visual where force, subject, typography, and composition all move together.
```

## 例 735：琐碎紧急情况漫画风海报设计

**来源：** [@VigoCreativeAI](https://x.com/VigoCreativeAI/status/2091888379604373647) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/small-emergencies-manga-ink-poster)

![case735.jpg](images/case735.jpg)

```text
{
  "style_name": "Small Emergencies",
  "style_slug": "manga-ink-three-panel-shift-poster",
  "canvas": "9:16 tall poster, aged cream paper, heavy print grain, distressed edges and corners",
  "color": {
    "ink": "near-black line work #1A1714",
    "paper": "cream #EFE3CE",
    "fills": ["hot pink #E85D8A", "sky cyan #6FC3D6", "signal yellow #E8B33A", "copper orange #D96B2E"],
    "rule": "flat, slightly misregistered print fills; pink must take ONE impossible role per poster — the water spray, the dawn sky, the ghosted second self"
  },
  "illustration": {
    "technique": "dense seinen-manga inking — crosshatch shading, speed lines, spray dots, screentone patches — over flat poster color",
    "panels": "three stacked panels separated by TILTED diagonal gutters with thin paper-colored gaps",
    "panel_grammar": {
      "instrument": "extreme close-up of a hand on the job's instrument — tuning dial, lantern striker, pressure gauge, kitchen timer — markings readable",
      "action": "the same worker full-body at the peak of a small crisis, strong diagonal pose, environment drawn in full detail",
      "face": "tight portrait of the same character — effort, relief or joy; optionally a ghosted second emotion behind them in one flat color"
    },
    "order_rule": "the three beats may shuffle order, but all three must appear, and it is the same character in every panel",
    "diegetic_text": "all in-scene words belong to the world — tape labels, shop signs, flour bags, buoy numbers, warning plates"
  },
  "typography": {
    "masthead": "two words, TWO typefaces: word one in massive distressed slab caps in cream; word two in ornate circus tuscan, set on or filled with a color block",
    "info_bar": "one yellow bar in bold black caps at the foot: place or season, slash, log or batch number",
    "scatter": "checkerboard patches, lightning bolts, starbursts, tiny waveforms — three or four per poster, never more"
  },
  "design_rules": [
    "the crisis must be genuinely minor — a relay to run, a lamp to light, a pipe to fix, dough to catch; the inking treats it as life or death",
    "the face panel pays off the action panel — same shift, seconds apart",
    "panels tilt; the masthead never does",
    "no gradients in the fills; depth comes from line density, not tone"
  ],
  "swap_variables": {
    "job": "dawn radio courier / pier storm-watch keeper / irrigation mechanic / night baker",
    "crisis": "carry the signal across rooftops / light the storm lantern in rain / burst pipe in dry season / dough over-rising at 3am",
    "instrument": "tuning dial / lantern + striker / gauge + pipe wrench / timer + copper bowl",
    "masthead": "STATIC RUN / TIDELIGHT / BLOOM FIX / MOON RISE",
    "info_bar": "FREQUENCY 88 · DAWN RELAY / PIER NINE · STORM WATCH / DRY SEASON · REPAIR LOG 04 / BATCH 03 · OPEN TILL DAWN",
    "diegetic_labels": "88 DAWN RELAY tape / buoy No.9 / WATER PRESSURE dial / ALL NIGHT BAKERY sign + POWER FLICKER plate"
  }
}
```

## 例 736：PHO REPUBLIC 越南粉 2x2 旗舰平面广告系列

**来源：** [@ou_zhen599](https://x.com/ou_zhen599/status/2092155004761285113) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/pho-republic-flagship-2x2-campaign-poster)

![case736.jpg](images/case736.jpg)

```text
Create a flagship 2x2 campaign poster series for an original Vietnamese noodle brand named "PHO REPUBLIC", merging Orbit energy with Transit restraint. Use ultra-realistic commercial food photography, not illustration, not 3D render. The final artwork must present four coordinated square posters arranged in a strict 2x2 grid, each panel built from the same structural system with disciplined variation. The pho bowl must remain the absolute visual hero in every panel.

Style lock: high-end graphic food advertising, bold editorial poster layout, product-first hierarchy, clean color-block backgrounds, oversized kinetic typography, youthful premium Asian street-dining branding, Cannes-level print campaign execution.

Core campaign structure: four square panels with alternating deep navy blue and warm mustard yellow backgrounds. Each panel contains one large white pho bowl shown from the exact same slightly top-down camera angle. Bowl size, bowl height, bowl perspective, rim thickness, bowl diameter, and visual weight must be perfectly consistent across all four posters. The container must sit at an equal visual scale in every panel, acting as the fixed campaign anchor. Variation is allowed only in typography rhythm, circular text paths, callout placement, and directional movement around the bowl.

Transit logic: simplify every panel so the layout feels flagship and globally polished. Reduce small labels, reduce decorative callouts, shorten copy, preserve more clean negative space, and let the bowl, background block, and primary typography do most of the work. Avoid flyer-like density. Each square should feel graphic, premium, and instantly readable.

Orbit logic: create a coordinated movement system across the full 2x2 set. Panel one uses a strong descending diagonal text flow, panel two uses a mirrored inward-driving diagonal, panel three uses a controlled circular orbit around the bowl, and panel four uses a tighter circular return path that visually resolves the campaign. The four panels should feel like one connected visual sequence: launch, counter, orbit, resolve. Motion must feel crisp and graphic, not noisy.

Food design: create a premium beef pho hero dish with richly detailed ingredients: glowing amber broth, thin rice noodles, sliced beef, beef balls, tendon or tripe accents, basil leaves, scallions, red chili slices, onion, fresh herbs, and subtle broth sheen. Ingredient styling may shift slightly between panels, but the bowl size and bowl dominance must never change. The dish must feel abundant, fragrant, hot, and highly craveable.

Panel logic:
1. Top-left panel: deep navy background, oversized stacked headline moving diagonally from upper-left toward center, bowl anchored in the lower-middle zone, a few restrained callout markers near the rim.
2. Top-right panel: mustard background, mirrored bold typography driving inward from upper-right toward center seam, bowl anchored at the same scale and height as panel one, minimal graphic accents only.
3. Bottom-left panel: mustard background, centered bowl with a clean circular text orbit around it, fewer callouts than the top row, stronger negative space.
4. Bottom-right panel: deep navy background, centered bowl matching panel three exactly in scale and placement, tighter orbit text and a compact brand footer block to conclude the campaign.

Typography and branding: use original English text only, never copy any existing wording. Use a bold master phrase such as "TALK THE PHO" as the campaign anchor across the series, varied in scale and rhythm but always consistent in brand voice. Add only a few short descriptors such as "fresh", "slow broth", "street soul", "herbal", and "comfort". Include a compact brand block reading "PHO REPUBLIC" with a subtitle such as "modern Vietnamese kitchen". Typography should feel oversized, architectural, witty, and kinetic, but always secondary to the bowls.

Lighting: bright controlled studio food lighting with soft frontal illumination and subtle side shaping. The broth must glow warmly, herbs must feel crisp and fresh, meat textures must remain moist and realistic, and the white ceramic bowls must look clean, glossy, and dimensional. Keep shadows soft and controlled, with no muddy contrast.

Materials and realism: realistic white ceramic bowls with identical shape and scale across all four posters, hot glossy broth, tender beef textures, springy noodles, fresh basil, crisp chili slices, subtle oil shimmer, clean matte background fields, premium print-ready sharpness, and integrated graphic elements that feel designed rather than pasted.

Color system: strict alternating navy blue and mustard yellow fields, with restrained accents of red, white, black, and herb green. Keep the palette disciplined, high-contrast, and premium. Food colors must remain natural and appetizing.

Quality and physical rules: all four panels must feel like one unified flagship campaign. Equal bowl size across every panel is mandatory. Equal bowl camera angle, equal bowl scale, equal bowl visual weight, and equal product dominance must be preserved. Keep the campaign clean, graphic, premium, and highly legible. No clutter, no warped rims, no type collisions, no weak food detail, no dead black patches, no oversaturated broth.

Negative prompt: copied branding, copied text, inconsistent bowl sizes, inconsistent bowl height, inconsistent bowl perspective, different container scale between panels, warped bowl rims, blurry food, fake broth texture, messy layout, chaotic typography, cheap flyer look, low-resolution print feel, oversaturated food colors, excessive labels, cluttered callouts, cartoon rendering, CGI look, illustration style, product losing dominance, muddy shadows, dirty background.
```

## 例 738：影像衍生品牌包装视觉识别海报

**来源：** [@xiaoxiaodong01](https://x.com/xiaoxiaodong01/status/2091477071751262459) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/brand-visual-identity-presentation-board)

![case738.jpg](images/case738.jpg)

```text
Please turn each photo I upload into an independent high-end design poster, no multi-photo stitching, each photo output individually. The overall composition uses a 3:4 vertical layout, with the upper and lower areas strictly 1:1 in height, each occupying 50% of the frame. The upper half preserves the original photo, maintaining the subject identity, structure, posture, realistic texture, natural lighting, and original color atmosphere, with only slight high-end photographic color grading to give it the texture of art magazines, independent publications, and exhibition photography. To adapt to the frame, the sky, ground, or environmental background can be naturally extended, but the subject must not be stretched, distorted, or changed. The lower half extracts the most recognizable subject, outline, posture, and narrative relationship from the photo, transforming it into a complete brand packaging and material visual system. Do not simply copy the photo; instead, refine the subject's characteristics into reusable core graphics, illustration symbols, patterns, color blocks, or identification elements, and then apply them to packaging carriers of different sizes and purposes, making all materials instantly belong to the same visual family. Automatically select the most suitable 5–8 carriers based on the original image's theme, such as paper bags, packaging boxes, gift sets, labels, tags, tickets, cards, stickers, sleeves, cup packaging, key pendants, wrapping paper, or other related materials, without forcing the use of fixed categories. Use 1–2 main packages as the visual focus, with the remaining materials forming modular combinations based on size, horizontal/vertical ratios, and function; use a clear grid, edge alignment, scale contrast, spacing rhythm, and large areas of white space to organize the frame, like a professional brand design studio's packaging identity presentation board, rather than product displays or messy mockup collages. The color palette remains soft, transparent, and slightly desaturated in high-end tones, mainly light pink-blue, mist blue, sky blue, and airy cool-toned blue, paired with ivory white, cream white, light beige, soft gray-green, and architectural neutral colors, using only a small amount of dusty rose or muted blush as visual accents. Different packaging can change the color area and combination method, but must maintain the same color system to form a rich yet unified sense of series. The material emphasizes realistic and exquisite paper and packaging craftsmanship, showing matte paper, fine-grained paper, thick cardstock, translucent paper, embossing, die-cutting, folding, stickers, partial printing, and slight layering relationships; lighting is soft and natural, only used to express paper thickness, creases, and material differences, avoiding plastic feel, strong reflections, exaggerated 3D, and cheap product rendering. Text is also designed uniformly as part of the entire packaging system. Extract a short English main title from the subject's name, theme, mood, function, or symbolic meaning of the original image, and extend a very small amount of sub-titles, short sentences, numbers, category names, or micro-descriptions. Text can change naturally according to different carriers: vertically arranged on tags, extending along packaging edges, surrounding circular labels, crossing box surfaces, hidden between graphics, or becoming part of the packaging closure structure, so that fonts, graphics, and materials together form a complete brand language, rather than repeating the same title on every item. The overall presentation is a high-end brand packaging system / visual identity proposal board: rich but not crowded, lively but orderly, showing both the complete series and the design logic between graphics, text, materials, and different carriers. Whether the original image is a person, animal, plant, architecture, object, food, vehicle, or natural landscape, a unique packaging language should be established from the original image itself, avoiding fixed product combinations, repetitive templates, e-commerce display feel, and ordinary brand mockup templates.
```

## 例 739：复古火柴盒标签风格旅行海报

**来源：** [@Hamburgerai](https://x.com/Hamburgerai/status/2093178203959627903) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/vintage-matchbox-travel-label-poster)

![case739.jpg](images/case739.jpg)

```text
Please turn each travel photo I upload into an individual high-end design poster. One photo corresponds to one finished product. No multi-photo collages, and do not create product display mockups.

Adopt a 3:4 vertical composition overall. The image consists of a real photography area at the top and a vintage label re-rendering area at the bottom, with a clear, straight horizontal boundary between the two areas.

The upper photography area accounts for about 45%–50% of the entire image. Faithfully preserve the subject, architecture, vehicles, terrain, direction, spatial relationships, observation angles, natural light and shadow, real textures, and color atmosphere of the original photo. Perform only slight high-end travel photography color grading and extremely thin film grain processing. The top must remain a clearly identifiable real photo; do not redraw, deform, mirror, replace subjects, or modify architecture.

The bottom accounts for about 50%–55%, re-rendering the same scene as a "VINTAGE MATCHBOX TRAVEL LABEL".

In a warm off-white or old-paper-colored empty field, place a flat rectangular old paper travel label. The label occupies about 48%–68% of the width of the lower area, with about 32%–52% continuous paper white space remaining around it.

Only show the front of the label; do not show matchbox thickness, drawers, sides, matches, packaging structures, handheld scenes, or product displays.

Extract the most recognizable subject outlines, orientations, postures, key architectural structures, and iconic color blocks from the original photo, compressing the complex environment into a central stamp, badge, or large color block composition.

The subject must be immediately traceable to the original photo, but photo tracing or automatic vectorization cannot be used. Reorganize the composition based on the subject's characteristics, for example:

- Landmark buildings compressed into thick-outline badges;
- Bridges transformed into strong directional structures of towers and cables;
- Roads transformed into exaggerated perspective wedges;
- Vehicles transformed into side-profile stamps composed of spoilers, bodies, and wheels;
- Mountains and terrain transformed into a few hard-edged silhouettes.

Use heavy near-black borders, thick black outlines, hard-edged flat painting, and 2–4 opaque spot colors. Colors must be extracted from the original photo, then summarized and moderately purified, while adding warm paper color and near-black outline color.

Express old color printing characteristics:

- coarse halftone;
- waxy ink grain;
- missing ink;
- rough printed edges;
- slight color misregistration;
- worn corners;
- aged paper fibers;
- uneven spot-color coverage.

Legible paper white space must still be reserved inside the label; do not fill the surroundings with decorations, dots, or borders.

Text is added as part of the label printing:

- A location or scene title of 1–3 English words;
- A number in the style of "LABEL NO. 01";
- An observation sentence of 5–10 English words.

Use bold, slightly irregular vintage uppercase fonts for the main title, and small old lead type or typewriter fonts for auxiliary text. Allow for slight ink missing, wear, and color registration offset, but the main title must be clearly readable in chat previews.

Do not copy trademarks, window stickers, organization names, author signatures, or irrelevant text from the original photo. Do not imitate real match brands, specific old packaging, or existing product labels.

The overall look is like a travel print preserved from an old suitcase, roadside shop, or local souvenir album: direct, coarse, sun-aged, and collectible, but not childish, cheap, or a nostalgic template.

Avoid: 3D matchboxes, sides of packaging boxes, matches, product advertisement mockups, real brand imitations, modern minimalist logos, smooth vectors, digital gradients, photographic filters, automatic photo tracing, too many colors, complex panoramas, children's cartoons, generic travel icons, redrawing of the entire poster, missing original photos, shrinking photos into small decorations, changing materials for the same framing, blurred boundaries, author names, logos, watermarks, gibberish, fictional brands, and redundant text.
```

## 例 740：南半球小众早餐文化对比海报提示词

**来源：** [@Gdgtify](https://x.com/Gdgtify/status/2093324721693868044) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/southern-hemisphere-underrated-breakfast-poster-prompt)

![case740.jpg](images/case740.jpg)

```text
2x2 grid, 16:9, do this for 4 under rated breakfasts of Southern hemisphere: INPUT = [TOPIC_CATEGORY / REGION_SET / SELECTION_CRITERIA / MOOD]
MODE = no_hardcoding / inference_only

Run REGIONAL_CULTURAL_ATLAS_EXTRACTION:

CATEGORY_DNA := infer what type of thing is being compared, how it is used, why it matters, and what makes it visually recognizable

REGION_DNA[each] := infer local environment, visual culture, palette, typography attitude, textures, symbols, landscapes, tools, rituals, and social context

ITEM_SELECTION[each] := infer one overlooked or representative item that fits TOPIC_CATEGORY + REGION + SELECTION_CRITERIA

HERO_VISUAL[each] := infer the best visual treatment:
plated food, product close-up, ritual scene, tool layout, garment portrait, object still life, landscape-backed artifact, map-backed diagram, or symbolic vignette

COPY_SYSTEM[each] := generate:
- large item title
- short emotional tagline
- origin/location label
- 2–4 tiny descriptors
- stamp or badge phrase
- small contextual note

GRAPHIC_SYSTEM := infer torn paper, printed grid, muted color blocks, topographic lines, stamps, borders, halftone, atlas marks, and editorial spacing

Render:
A premium regional comparison poster where each panel feels locally specific but belongs to one unified cultural theme.

Forbidden:
hard-coded Southern Hemisphere, fixed breakfasts, fixed foods, fixed countries, fixed beige/red/green palette, fake local facts, random travel-poster clichés.
```

## 例 741：极简栅格线条实验性海报

**来源：** [@MrLarus](https://x.com/MrLarus/status/2093001886156673349) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/experimental-raster-line-poster-design)

![case741.jpg](images/case741.jpg)

```text
[SUBJECT]: {subject}
[TITLE]: {name}
[BACKGROUND]: {color}
[LINES]: {color}

[TEXT]: {2–4 short lines}
[FORMAT]: 9:16

Create a refined experimental poster built from a continuous field of fine parallel raster lines.

Make [SUBJECT] feel like a hidden 3D form pushing through the line field. The subject should emerge mainly through smooth compression, expansion, bending, and negative space — not through a separate outline or realistic rendering.

Keep the subject at roughly 70–80% of the composition and use only a few large, controlled deformation zones. Away from the subject, the lines should gradually return to straight parallel form.

Prioritize line quality above everything else: extremely fine, sharp, smooth, evenly spaced, and uninterrupted curves.

Add 2–3 small typography blocks inside quieter negative spaces using minimal modern sans-serif type.

Style: Op Art, generative graphic design, experimental editorial poster, modern sculpture, refined minimalism.

Avoid broken or intersecting lines, jagged curves, chaotic ripples, excessive realism, wireframe outlines, sci-fi UI, excessive text, logos, numbering, starbursts, and sparkles.
```

## 例 742：现代社论拼贴海报艺术提示词

**来源：** [@MrLarus](https://x.com/MrLarus/status/2093153744879849723) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/modern-editorial-collage-poster-art-prompt)

![case742.jpg](images/case742.jpg)

```text
Theme: {e.g. Fashion / Music / Running / Art}
Subject: {describe the person, outfit, pose, expression, or object}
Headline: {main title}
Supporting text: {2–4 short meaningful phrases}
Primary colors: {2–3 colors}
Mood: {cool / energetic / hypnotic / quiet / etc.}
Aspect ratio: {9:16 / 4:5 / 3:4}

Create a sophisticated editorial collage poster combining realistic photography, color-shifted portrait treatment, bold geometric graphics, and modern magazine typography.

Use a clean white or lightly textured warm-ivory background with generous negative space.

Make one photographic subject the main visual anchor, occupying roughly 50–70% of the composition. Give the subject a clear personality, natural body language, and a strong emotional state rather than a generic model pose.

Treat the photographic subject itself as part of the color system. Use selective duotone and multitone color mapping across different parts of the face, hair, clothing, and body.

Allow some areas to remain neutral and photographic while other areas naturally shift into the chosen colors.

Keep facial highlights and skin tones clean, realistic, and flattering. Introduce stronger color mainly through shadows, clothing, edges, and selected overlapping areas. Avoid muddy, dirty, or unnatural skin tones.

Add 2–4 bold geometric elements such as cropped circles, arches, rectangles, organic shapes, color slices, or open frames.

The graphics should actively interact with the subject through masking, cropping, overlapping, and color transfer. When a colored shape crosses the portrait, let the photographic tones beneath it shift naturally, creating screen-print, risograph, or offset-print color interactions rather than looking like a flat transparent sticker.

Optional: add a second cropped photo detail, subtle portrait echo, registration-shift effect, halftone texture, line pattern, or theme-related graphic element.

Use a restrained color system: light neutral background, 2–3 strong colors, dark neutral typography.

Create a clear editorial typography hierarchy with one dominant headline and only a few short supporting phrases. All text should have real meaning related to the theme.

Keep the composition asymmetrical, layered, energetic, and intentionally art-directed while preserving enough empty space to avoid visual clutter.

The final image should feel like a contemporary independent fashion, music, sports, or culture magazine poster with expressive photography and experimental print-inspired color treatment.
```

## 例 744：笔触涂鸦插画海报生成提示词

![case744.jpg](images/case744.jpg)

```text
Transform the attached photo into a brush-painted illustration poster. The subject in the photo can be food, another product, a landscape, an animal, a person, or any object you want to feature as the main subject.
[Aspect Ratio]
Vertical 3:4
[Style]
Digital painting / brush-painted effect.
Visible brush strokes, uneven edges, slightly stretched paint marks.
Hand-painted appearance that feels untouched and authentic.
Interpret the image as a flat illustration with a simple arrangement of shapes rather than a realistic depiction.
No gradients, shadows, 3D rendering, or detailed textures. Keep everything completely flat.
Use a thin, irregular brush-painted outline that varies slightly from the painted areas.
[Color]
Background should be cream / off-white paper.
Use only 3–4 colors taken from the attached photo:
1 dark primary color
1 bright primary color
1–2 supporting colors
Slightly desaturated vintage tone.
Never increase the number of colors.
[Composition]
The subject should be large and centered, occupying approximately 50–55% of the canvas.
Extend the composition vertically with more space above and below, while also increasing the left and right margins.
At the top: place 1–2 English uppercase words describing the subject, in very large hand-painted brush lettering. Use very wide letter spacing; the text may wrap around the illustration or extend toward the right/downward.
At the bottom right: add a short handwritten English phrase of 3–5 words in an accent color.
[Text]
Look at the photo and choose suitable top and bottom phrases.
Do not use the actual subject name as the top text.
Choose words based on the atmosphere, location, season, time of day, or emotion shown in the photo.
The bottom phrase should feel casual, lighthearted, and slightly playful—not overly serious or explanatory.
[Forbidden]
Realistic rendering
3D
Shadows
Reflections
Logos
Watermarks
Border frames
Excessive enlargement of the subject
```

## 例 747：奢华现代别墅模型房地产销售海报

**来源：** [@ou_zhen599](https://x.com/ou_zhen599/status/2090338483592368349) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/auren-casa-premium-vertical-real-estate-poster)

![case747.jpg](images/case747.jpg)

```text
Create a premium vertical real-estate sales poster for a fictional boutique developer brand called AUREN CASA, refined with stronger Orbit reveal energy and more polished Transit luxury. Preserve the original structural logic exactly: a soft warm beige luxury interior background in shallow depth of field, a large central modern villa maquette floating between two realistic human hands, the upper hand lifting a thin roof slab upward, the lower hand supporting the architectural model from beneath, a clean logo-and-headline zone at the top, and a compact branded contact-information strip at the bottom. The whole image must feel like a flagship property-launch visual: elegant, emotionally warm, and internationally premium.

Main visual:
At the center, place one ultra-refined two-story modern villa maquette with fully visible cutaway interiors. Strengthen the Orbit direction by making the lifted roof plane and the supported house body feel more dynamically related, as if the act of opening the roof is actively releasing the living experience inside. The gap between roof slab and model should feel deliberate and slightly more dramatic, while remaining realistic and controlled. The house model must feel handcrafted, valuable, and architecturally believable: slim dark metal frame, floor-to-ceiling glass, warm walnut or oak surfaces, recessed lighting, elegant living room, dining zone, upper private suite, indoor greenery, and clean layered floor plates.

Architecture and interiors:
The villa must read as a premium product, not a conceptual toy. Lower floor: open-plan living and dining with refined furniture, warm shelves, art piece, and subtle residential luxury. Upper floor: softer intimate bedroom or lounge suite. Materials must be rendered with great care: timber, glass, matte slabs, metal framing, upholstery texture, ambient light glow, and miniature lifestyle elegance. The interior should feel warm, inviting, and globally premium.

Hands and realism:
Both hands must be realistic, elegant, and anatomically correct, with natural finger spacing, believable grip pressure, fine skin texture, and beauty-commercial lighting. The upper hand should feel graceful and intentional, delicately lifting the roof slab. The lower hand should feel stable and supportive, reinforcing craftsmanship and care. No hand distortion, no extra fingers, no awkward pose.

Transit direction:
Refine the whole page toward a more international, more editorial, and more luxurious launch-poster standard. Reduce graphic noise, keep the top branding cleaner, make the contact strip more understated, soften any excessive contrast in the background blur, and allow the central model to carry more of the emotional and commercial weight. The image should feel like a flagship collaboration between a top-tier developer and a premium interior design house.

Lighting and mood:
Use soft warm daylight with subtle architectural interior glow. Let the model receive elegant directional light from upper-right or upper-front, with delicate highlights on the roof slab, glass edges, timber interiors, and the hands. Strengthen light-shadow relationships enough to give the model genuine volume and make the lifted-roof action feel more dramatic. The background should remain creamy, blurred, and luxurious, with no distraction.

Typography and branding:
Top zone: one original minimal logo mark, one original developer name, and one short English headline such as “OPEN TO ELEVATED LIVING” or “WHERE SPACE BECOMES HOME,” fully original and not copied. Add one small supporting line beneath.  
Bottom strip: one elegant branded contact bar with original fictional phone number, city text, and website, cleaner and more restrained than a brochure banner.  
All typography must be original, modern, premium, and secondary to the architectural model.

Style:
Luxury real-estate launch poster, premium architecture-and-interior branding, boutique residential campaign, design-build property marketing, hyper-real commercial rendering with emotional warmth, Cannes-level developer advertising polish.

Color system:
Warm beige, soft taupe, cream, walnut brown, muted charcoal frame lines, amber interior glow, restrained dark text, and one subtle brand accent if needed. Keep everything calm, polished, and globally premium.

Negative prompt:
no copied brand names, no copied slogans, no cluttered background, no toy-house feel, no distorted roof geometry, no warped hands, no extra fingers, no malformed anatomy, no muddy shadows, no black blotches, no watermark, no existing logos, no noisy layout, no cheap brochure feel.
```

## 例 748：AURELIA LAND云曜天际房产海报设计

**来源：** [@ou_zhen599](https://x.com/ou_zhen599/status/2090350708570955863) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/aurelia-land-the-address-above-luxury-poster)

![case748.jpg](images/case748.jpg)

```text
Create a premium vertical flagship real-estate advertising poster for a fictional luxury property developer brand called AURELIA LAND, promoting its iconic serviced-residence tower project “云曜天际” with the English subtitle “The Address Above”. The image must merge Orbit-style upward momentum with Transit-style editorial restraint, while making the brand memory unmistakably strong through a singular architectural identity, a disciplined logo system, and a high-recognition visual symbol. The building must remain the absolute visual hero.

Core concept:
A refined luxury high-rise tower rises from a razor-thin dark premium platform resting on a realistic adult human hand in the lower foreground. The hand symbolizes ownership, elevation, and control, but remains secondary. The main brand memory device is the tower itself: it must have a distinctive signature silhouette with elegant stepped balcony bands and a recognizable crown profile, so the architecture becomes an instantly identifiable brand icon. The entire poster should feel like one unforgettable commercial symbol: a branded tower held above the city.

Composition:
Vertical poster, highly centralized composition, strong symmetry with subtle premium asymmetry in light and skyline depth. The tower stands at exact center, occupying the visual core and extending upward into generous negative space. The platform is thin, dark, glossy, and engineered, acting like a luxury pedestal-device hybrid. A realistic hand supports the platform from below, emerging from the lower edge with correct anatomy and understated presence. The upper half contains a tightly controlled premium typography system with clear brand hierarchy. The lower section contains only a reduced, elegant information structure. No clutter.

Orbit direction:
Strengthen the feeling of ascent and elevation. The tower should feel as if it is being presented upward toward the sky, with balcony lines, facade rhythm, lighting gradation, and podium geometry all guiding the eye vertically. The silhouette should become slimmer and more luminous toward the upper levels. Palm trees, podium lighting, and platform perspective should subtly reinforce an upward lift. The visual energy is not chaotic; it is a calm, prestigious rise.

Transit restraint:
Keep the layout extremely clean and premium. Remove all unnecessary decorative elements, excessive text blocks, or busy urban distractions. The skyline in the background is soft, blurred, and minimal. The poster should read in one second: brand, tower, aspiration. Typography must be sparse, measured, and elegant. Every element should feel expensive, intentional, and globally marketable.

Architecture:
An internationally upscale 14-to-16-story luxury serviced-residence tower with a highly memorable form: sculpted stepped terraces, continuous wraparound balconies, warm interior light, floor-to-ceiling low-iron glass, champagne-bronze frame accents, pale limestone or travertine surfaces, transparent corner glazing, and a refined hospitality-style podium with double-height lobby glass. The crown should have a distinctive final-tier expression, elegant but instantly recognizable. The tower must look architecturally credible, developer-grade, investment-worthy, and beautifully buildable.

Brand identity emphasis:
Create a strong, cohesive visual branding system. At the very top, place a minimal geometric logo mark derived from the tower’s silhouette, paired with the brand name AURELIA LAND in a refined luxury wordmark. Introduce one restrained signature brand accent color, muted champagne gold, repeated only in the logo mark, one key headline word, and a thin divider line, so the identity becomes memorable without becoming noisy. The tower silhouette, logo form, and balcony rhythm should feel like one unified brand language. The image must not feel like a generic property ad; it must feel like a proprietary flagship campaign.

Typography and layout:
Use original, premium, restrained text only. Top: the AURELIA LAND logo system. Below: project name “云曜天际”. Main Chinese headline: “一座被记住的天际资产”. Smaller English line: “A landmark shaped for prestige, return, and recognition.” Add one ultra-minimal descriptor line beneath: “Serviced residences | Elevated lifestyle | Prime long-term value”. At the bottom, reduce to three concise icon-led selling points such as “Iconic Skyline Presence”, “Private Hospitality Living”, “Prime Urban Growth”, followed by a clean, narrow contact strip. Typography should be spacious, balanced, high-end, and clearly subordinate to the architecture.

Lighting:
Golden sunset transitioning into early dusk, with soft radiant sky light, warm architectural interior glow, controlled highlights on metal trims and balcony slabs, and subtle rim light around the tower silhouette. The light should help the tower separate cleanly from the background and amplify its brand-icon status. Strong but elegant tonal hierarchy, no muddy shadows, no blown sky, no dead-black blocks.

Material rendering:
Ultra-real architectural detail: low-iron glass with soft realistic reflections, champagne-bronze brushed metal edges, matte pale stone facade surfaces, warm glowing lobby interiors, refined balcony undersides, polished dark platform edge, realistic tropical greenery, and a photoreal hand with fine pores, natural skin folds, realistic tendons, subtle joint definition, correct finger anatomy, and believable sunset reflection. The building must never feel toy-like or miniature; it must feel like a real luxury tower presented through a surreal but premium advertising gesture.

Environment:
A calm high-end metropolitan skyline at sunset, softly blurred and low-contrast, with a few elegant tower silhouettes in the far distance. Minimal palm trees and restrained podium landscaping around the building base. No cars, no crowds, no unnecessary props, no excessive city detail. The environment exists only to elevate the branded tower symbol.

Color system:
Warm ivory, pale sunset gold, champagne metallic accents, limestone beige, charcoal-black platform tones, warm amber interior lights, muted palm green, and faint blue-grey city haze. Saturation is controlled and premium. The gold accent should feel proprietary and memorable, not loud.

Style:
Cannes-level luxury real-estate campaign, flagship developer launch visual, ultra-clean international branding, iconic architecture-as-brand-symbol thinking, hyper-real architectural visualization fused with premium advertising photography, elegant, aspirational, memorable, investment-oriented.

Image quality and physical rules:
Ultra-detailed, sharp tower edges, coherent perspective, realistic structural depth, believable hand-to-platform scale, accurate lighting direction, clean skyline falloff, realistic glass behavior, no floating geometry, no warped balconies, no broken fingers, no cheap brochure styling, no text overload.

Negative prompt:
generic developer branding, copied original slogan, copied original logo, weak brand identity, cheap brochure layout, cluttered typography, toy-like miniature tower, warped architecture, fake CGI materials, plastic facade, overdecorated luxury style, dead black shadows, muddy sunset, oversaturated gold, noisy skyline, extra hands, deformed fingers, missing fingers, fused fingers, broken anatomy, floating building, unreadable icons, messy information bar, random city clutter, watermark, real person names
```


---

## 例 749：实验性社论海报与现代排版摄影设计

**来源：** [@MrLarus](https://x.com/MrLarus/status/2090320559389192446) / [OpenNana](https://opennana.com/awesome-prompt-gallery/experimental-editorial-poster-photography-design)

![case749.jpg](images/case749.jpg)

```text
[SUBJECT]: {person / architecture / everyday object / lifestyle scene}
[TITLE]: {one short keyword}
[THEME]: {what the image represents}
[SCENE]: {city / field / street corner / architectural space}
[ACCENT COLOR]: {red / blue / orange / etc.}
[SHORT LINE]: {one meaningful sentence}
[FORMAT]: 9:16

Create an experimental editorial poster combining photography, oversized typography, and subtle print texture.

Use {SUBJECT} as the main visual inside {SCENE}. Keep it realistic, distinctive, and large enough to hold the composition. The subject should feel like a real cultural or everyday observation, not a generic stock image.

Set {TITLE} in oversized bold condensed sans-serif type. Do not simply place the title over the image — let the subject and typography overlap, interrupt, frame, or pass behind each other so they become one composition. The typography can respond naturally to the subject’s shape, direction, or architecture.

Use a warm off-white, concrete gray, or deep charcoal base with subtle paper grain and slightly aged print texture. Keep most of the palette neutral and use {ACCENT COLOR} only in a few small lines, blocks, or graphic marks.

Add restrained editorial details such as thin guide lines, diagrams, maps, grids, annotations, or geometric elements only when they support the subject. Keep the layout rich but controlled, with clear hierarchy and intentional negative space.

Keep all supporting text meaningful and minimal. Avoid random placeholder words, excessive technical data, unnecessary numbering, logos, watermarks, sparkles, and decorative clutter.

Overall style: contemporary editorial design × photographic collage × experimental typography × subtle neo-retro print aesthetics.
```
